> Computed 2026-09-24 from [[Hit or Miss - Miss]] against this course's own weights and policies. Maximum grade still reachable if everything remaining is aced — not a real transcript grade.
- **Journal Entries (30%, 12 entries, 0–2 scale, 2.5% per entry at full 2 pts):** Journal 1 and 3 both submitted late — each drops from 2/2 to 1/2 on the rubric, costing half its share. **−2.5%** (1.25% × 2 entries).
- **Attendance — the real threat, not a point deduction:** 2 cumulative weeks already missed. This course's own policy: **more than 1 cumulative week unexcused lowers the grade** (no stated numeric amount), and **3 cumulative weeks unexcused fails the course outright**. You are one missed week away from an automatic F, independent of every point total below.
**Ceiling: ~97.5%** on points alone — but that number is close to meaningless while the attendance threshold sits one absence away from a hard override.
## Source of Truth
> [!IMPORTANT] Read before trusting anything about this course
> Real source folder: `D:\_Anant\10_Areas\UMN\Classes\Lib Eds & More\ENGL 1004` - syllabus, slides, readings, and any Canvas exports land there first. This Board note is the readable distillation of the real syllabus PDF (read in full 2026-09-09) - not a replacement for checking Canvas directly, especially for journal-entry deadlines and any simulation-role updates.
> [!WARNING] Read this before enrolling attention gets assumed
> The syllabus opens with its own content warning, stated directly: "it is likely that you will find one or more of the class texts offensive and almost certain that you will find one or more uncomfortable and difficult to read... If you will be triggered by difficult, graphic, or offensive material in a way that will prohibit your class participation or completion of assignments you SHOULD NOT enroll in this course." Not boilerplate - the reading list includes *The Handmaid's Tale*, *The Bluest Eye*, and *Maus*, among others actually challenged/banned in real school districts.
## What This Course Actually Covers
A course about book banning, taught by reading the actual banned/challenged books alongside real First Amendment case law and state legislation. Not literary analysis in the abstract - the course culminates in a **live school-board simulation** where every student role-plays a real stakeholder (school board member, a parent aligned with Moms for Liberty, a community member opposed to any banning, a local reporter, a lawyer) arguing over three specific challenged titles: *And Tango Makes Three* (elementary read-aloud), *Melissa* (5th/6th grade classroom libraries), and *The Bluest Eye* (high school curriculum).
## Instructor & TA
**Professor Lianna Farber** (farbe004@umn.edu) - Pillsbury Hall 320I, office hours TTh 8:30–9:30am and by appointment.
**TA Sachiko Hamada** (hamad046@umn.edu) - Pillsbury Hall 220, office hours by appointment (T 1:30–3:00pm / Th 1:30–2:30pm).
## Meetings
**TTh 9:45–11:00am, Pillsbury Hall 214.**
## Required Texts
Eight books, plus Canvas/linked readings: Margaret Atwood, *The Handmaid's Tale*. Alison Bechdel, *Fun House*. Alex Gino, *Melissa* (originally published as *George* - either edition works). Toni Morrison, *The Bluest Eye*. Justin Richardson & Peter Parnell, *And Tango Makes Three*. Art Spiegelman, *Maus I and II*. Raina Telgemeier, *Drama*. Mark Twain, *Adventures of Huckleberry Finn*. A dedicated physical journal/notebook is also required (a 99-cent composition notebook is explicitly recommended) - journal entries must be handwritten, not typed.
## Class Requirements & Materials
Bring the assigned reading, a pen/pencil, and paper to every class. Electronic devices stay away unless specifically invited out. **Journal entries are due at least 1 hour before class** on assigned days, submitted as a photo or scan to Canvas.
## Grading
| Component | Weight |
|---|---|
| Journal Entries (12 total) | 30% |
| Role Creation | 10% |
| Simulation | 20% |
| Class Participation (excluding simulation) | 20% |
| Final Exam | 20% |
No extra credit is mentioned anywhere in this syllabus. Scale: standard UMN A (4.0) through F, S equivalent to C- or better - see Policies below for the full breakdown.
## Journal Entries
12 entries across the semester, handwritten, graded on a 0–2 scale per entry: **2** = well-considered, genuinely explores an issue the text raises; **1** = insufficiently considered, or late by up to a week; **0** = not submitted, or more than a week late. Early entries (1–3) are written "in your own voice" - real reactions with concrete textual evidence, not just feelings. Entries 4–6 and beyond shift to writing *as your assigned simulation character* - reasoning the way that character would, not as yourself.
## Role Creation & Simulation
Roles get assigned after a preference questionnaire, then fleshed out in class with a background and personal objectives - once set, **you're expected to stick to them** through the simulation. The simulation itself runs across three class sessions (10/8, 10/13, 10/20) as a school-board meeting on the three challenged titles, followed by a debrief on media reporting (10/22). Grading is based on how well you hold your objectives and how engaged you are - only students assigned to report on the proceedings may use a computer or electronic note-taking device during it.
## Class Participation
Explicitly **not the same as attendance** - graded on engagement and intellectual risk-taking. Being physically present but visibly disengaged scores a 0 for that day, same as an absence would for attendance purposes.
## Attendance
Mandatory, with an escalating penalty structure: missing more than **one cumulative week** unexcused lowers your grade; missing **three cumulative weeks** unexcused fails the course outright. Coming to class unprepared can get you asked to leave, counted as an unexcused absence.
## Electronic Devices
No use in class except when specifically invited. First violation gets a verbal warning; the **second violation is an automatic 0 for that day's participation grade** - stated as a deliberate policy, not an idle threat, with the instructor citing both anecdotal and research-based effects on retention.
## Final Exam
One in-class exam on the last scheduled day (**2026-12-15**) - passage identification from the readings, then discussion connecting the passage to the larger work and to the semester's discussions.

Since ISL and DLB are separate books with their own chapter numbering, notes live in `Textbook/ISL/Chapter - [N].md` and `Textbook/DLB/Chapter - [N].md` — not one folder, and not named by week, since a single chapter (e.g. ISL Ch2) gets assigned across multiple weeks in pieces and should accumulate into one note rather than fragment into several. # internship-research-loop — Git Clone and Bootstrap

## One-Line Answer

This is a Python 3.12 pipeline repo with no Node tooling and no `pyproject.toml`/`uv` — clone it, create a plain `.venv`, install `requirements.txt`, and run the test suite before touching anything else, to confirm the checkout (not just the clone command) is actually good.

## 1. Base WSL and GitHub CLI — shared, not repeated here

Already covered once for this machine in [[second-brain-claudekit-git-clone-and-bootstrap]] §1–3 (WSL base verification, `apt install git git-lfs curl jq ... gh`, `gh auth login --hostname github.com --web --git-protocol https`). Don't redo `gh auth login` per repo — it's a machine-level credential, not something this repo owns.

## 2. Clone the codebase

```bash
mkdir -p "$HOME/projects/work"
git clone https://github.com/gupta-builds/internship-research-loop.git "$HOME/projects/work/internship-research-loop"
cd "$HOME/projects/work/internship-research-loop"
git status --short --branch
```

If the destination already exists, inspect before cloning over it:

```bash
git -C "$HOME/projects/work/internship-research-loop" status --short --branch
git -C "$HOME/projects/work/internship-research-loop" remote -v
```

## 3. Python environment

This repo is now a uv project (converted 2026-09-27): `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` exist at the repo root, both committed. Python 3.12 is still pinned the same way as every GitHub Actions workflow, now also through `requires-python` in `pyproject.toml`.

```bash
uv sync
```

`uv sync` reads `uv.lock` and creates or updates `.venv` with the exact resolved versions, replacing the old `python3.12 -m venv .venv && pip install -r requirements.txt` sequence. `requirements.txt` still exists in the repo for reference, but `uv.lock` is now the source of truth for installed versions.

## 4. Validate the checkout

```bash
source .venv/bin/activate
python3 -m pytest -q
```

Expect **499 passed** (2026-09-26 baseline). A different count on a later checkout isn't itself alarming (the suite grows), but fewer tests or any failure means the checkout or environment is wrong, not the tests.

```bash
python3 -m py_compile core/*.py ingestion/*.py vault_writer/*.py \
  run_pipeline.py recheck.py reseed.py revalidate.py screen_report.py enrich.py
test -d .claude/agents && test -d .claude/skills
test -f CLAUDE.md && test -f AGENTS.md && test -f README.md && test -f PRD.md
```

## 5. Start the coding agent

```bash
cd "$HOME/projects/work/internship-research-loop"
claude
```

Or Codex from the same directory — `.codex/agents/*.toml` and `.codex/hooks.json` mirror `.claude/`'s agents and hooks for it (see `CLAUDE.md`'s note on why this is a mirror, not a second source of truth to hand-maintain).

## What this step does not set up

- The Jarvis vault connection (MCP servers, or a real sibling checkout for running the pipeline scripts by hand) — see [[internship-research-loop-jarvis-vault-and-secrets]].
- `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` / `JARVIS_DIR` for a manual local run of `run_pipeline.py`/`enrich.py` — same note.
- The two-laptop branch convention — not a setup step, just don't commit to `master` once this checkout exists; see `CLAUDE.md`'s "Two-laptop workflow" entry.

- [[internship-research-loop-new-laptop-directive]]
- [[internship-research-loop-jarvis-vault-and-secrets]]
- [[second-brain-claudekit-git-clone-and-bootstrap]]

# VS Code - WSL
## One-Line Answer
==A WSL window is the Windows VS Code UI driving a VS Code Server inside Ubuntu, so settings and keybindings come from Windows but every extension that runs code, every toolchain, every conda env and every Claude Code MCP entry has to exist again on the Linux side.== Main development happens here; installs are mirrored through [[VS Code - Install Loop]].
## How a WSL Window Works
The WSL extension starts a **VS Code Server** under `~/.vscode-server/bin/<commit>/` and talks to it over a random local port. UI extensions stay on Windows; workspace extensions (language servers, Python, Jupyter, Claude Code, Codex) run inside WSL and are installed there separately.
- *Right way to open:* `code .` from a WSL shell, `Ctrl+Alt+W`, or `WSL: Connect to WSL`. The window title shows `[WSL: Ubuntu-24.04]`.
- *Wrong way:* `\\wsl.localhost\...` from a Windows window, which runs Windows extensions and Windows git over 9P (happened three times before 2026-09-21 14:14).
- *Where code lives:* the Linux filesystem (`~/projects/...`). Codebases here are their own clones and meet Windows clones only through GitHub.
## File Map
| Path | What lives there |
|---|---|
| `~/.vscode-server/bin/<commit>/` | server build, matches the client (1.139.1, `04c0d99`) |
| `~/.vscode-server/extensions/` | WSL-side extensions (34) |
| `~/.vscode-server/data/Machine/settings.json` | Remote settings (machine-scoped values) |
| `~/.claude.json` | Claude Code user-scope MCP servers |
| `~/miniconda3/`, `~/.condarc`, `~/conda/specs/` | conda install, config, env specs |
| `~/.config/starship.toml` | prompt config, same file as Windows |
| `~/projects/{ai,hub,hackathon,scratch,work}` | codebases |
## Configured State, 2026-09-26
- *Extensions (34):* the shared working set plus `semgrep.semgrep` and `rust-lang.rust-analyzer`. Full list in [[VS Code - Install Loop]].
- *Remote settings:* bash as the default terminal, Python Environments activation in terminals, `python.condaPath` at `~/miniconda3/bin/conda`, Jupyter hides `/usr/bin/python3`, `/bin/python3` and conda base, watcher excludes for `.venv`, `node_modules`, `target`. Everything else is inherited from Windows.
- *Miniconda:* installed 2026-09-26 (conda 26.7.1) at `~/miniconda3`, conda-forge only, strict priority, `auto_activate` off, `changeps1` off, `conda init bash` added to `.bashrc`. Your decision puts miniconda wherever Jupyter runs, which replaces the WSL master plan's "Windows-only exception" line.
- *`jupyter-base`:* identical to Windows (Python 3.12.14, numpy 2.5.3, pandas 3.0.6, scikit-learn 1.9.1, plus ipykernel, ipywidgets, pyarrow, scipy, matplotlib, seaborn). Spec at `~/conda/specs/jupyter-base.yml`.
- *uv:* 0.12.17 in `~/.local/bin` for every non-notebook project.
- *MCP for Claude Code:* `jarvis`, `the-plan`, `jarvis-fs` at user scope (they work from any directory, verified from `/tmp`). The old `~/.mcp.json` is backed up as `~/.mcp.json.bak-20260926` and removed; its deprecated `server-github` entry was dropped.
- *Jarvis auth:* `JARVIS_API_KEY` reaches WSL through the Windows `WSLENV` variable. It applies to WSL shells started after 2026-09-26 14:05. The network path already worked (networking mode is mirrored), so the earlier failure was the missing key: HTTP 401 without it, 200 with it.
- *Docker:* WSL integration on, data at `D:\WSL\DockerDesktopWSL`, auto-start off. Start Docker Desktop before container work.
## Terminal Environment, Secrets and MCP Platforms, 2026-09-27
The gap this note's own `next:` field named ("port init.ps1 to init.sh") and the one [[VS Code - MCP and Secrets]] named ("port the registry to WSL") are both closed now, verified by running commands rather than assumed:
- *Terminal environment:* `~/.config/vscode-env/init.sh` is a bash port of `init.ps1`, same order (base, detect, header, workspace hook, warnings). Hooked from the end of `~/.bashrc`, guarded by `TERM_PROGRAM=vscode` and `VSCODE_ENV_DISABLE`. Detection uses only bash builtins and file tests on the hot path, no subprocess spawns, so it adds no measurable startup cost (median 0.52 s with or without it, 5 warm runs). A folder with `.envrc` is reported in the header and `.vscode/env.sh` is not also loaded, so direnv and this hook never fight over the same folder. Full measurements: [[VS Code - Terminal Environments]].
- *Task shells:* bash never sources `.bashrc` for a non-interactive, non-login shell, so a VS Code task gets none of this by default; confirmed directly (`bash -c` shows empty `$-` interactive flag and an unset marker variable). `BASH_ENV` would close the gap but runs on every non-interactive bash invocation on the machine, not just VS Code tasks, so it stays undone on purpose rather than being forced in.
- *Home workspace:* `~/.vscode/env.sh` and `~/.vscode/settings.json` are the Linux equivalents of the Windows home files: same status block (space on `/` and `/mnt/d`, Jarvis/The Plan reachability over a sub-200 ms TCP check, memory files, Docker socket, `~/projects` layout), same `mcp-sync`/`mcp-check`/`home-status` helper names, same secret-and-cache exclude lists adapted to Linux paths (`miniconda3`, `.vscode-server`, `.claude/projects`, in place of the Windows profile clutter). `~/.vscode` is not the WSL server's own folder (that is `~/.vscode-server`), so there is no extensions-folder collision the way there is on Windows.
- *Claude Code deny rules:* `~/.claude/settings.json` denies the same file classes as Windows (`.env*`, `.mcp.env`, `.credentials.json`, `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.azure`, `~/.kube`, `~/.codex/auth.json`), in Linux path form, plus two WSL-specific entries replacing the Windows-only credential script: `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml` and `~/.git-credentials` (WSL authenticates through `gh auth git-credential`, so this is the actual secret surface here). Verified: a dummy `/tmp` `.env` was blocked, a normal file beside it was read.
- *Global git ignore:* `~/.config/git/ignore` has the same eight patterns as the Windows file (`.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.*.local`, `.env.production`, `.mcp.env`, `.credentials.json`, `*.pem`, `*.key`). `core.excludesFile` stays unset on both sides; git's own default (`$HOME/.config/git/ignore`) picks it up with no config needed, confirmed with a scratch repo: `.env` and `.mcp.env` ignored, `.env.example` not.
- *MCP registry gets a `platforms` field:* `targets` already said which tool (claude/vscode/codex); `platforms` now says which OS side (default both if omitted). `jarvis-fs` is in the registry for the first time, `platforms: ["wsl"]`, using the same `npx @modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` command already live in Claude Code's WSL config, so applying it reports "in sync", not "update". `sync-mcp.ps1` skips anything not for windows and still reports jarvis/the-plan in sync on both Claude Code and VS Code.
- *WSL sync script:* `~/.config/mcp/sync-mcp.sh` reads the Windows registry directly through `/mnt/c` (never copies it) and applies to `claude` (`~/.claude.json` via `claude mcp add --scope user`) and `codex` (`codex mcp add --url --bearer-token-env-var`, http servers only, matching the Windows script's own limit). It refuses `vscode` (there is one VS Code user `mcp.json`, on Windows, read by both window types; writing it from WSL is how drift starts) and refuses `--set-secret` (secrets are set on Windows with `sync-mcp.ps1 -SetSecret` and reach WSL through `WSLENV`, already true for `JARVIS_API_KEY` and `THE_PLAN_API_KEY`). `cd /tmp && claude mcp list` shows `jarvis`, `the-plan` and `jarvis-fs` all connected.
- *User tasks:* `env: create workspace environment (.vscode/env.ps1 / env.sh)` and `mcp: sync registry to all tools` both have real `linux` commands now instead of the earlier "Windows-only task" placeholders, edited as text in the synced `tasks.json` so the file's own comments survived untouched.
## Toolchain State
| Tool | State |
|---|---|
| `git` | `core.autocrlf=false`, GitHub auth through `gh auth git-credential` |
| `uv` | 0.12.17 |
| `conda` | 26.7.1, `jupyter-base` env |
| `node` / `pnpm` | v24.21.0 via nvm in interactive shells |
| `gh`, `direnv`, `claude`, `semgrep`, `starship` 1.26.0 | installed |
| `docker` | works when Docker Desktop runs |
## Trust Layout
- *Trusted parents:* `~/projects/ai`, `~/projects/hub`, `~/projects/work`, `~/projects/hackathon`.
- *Landing zone:* `~/projects/scratch` stays untrusted.
- *Home:* `/home/anant_gupta` is not trusted as a parent.
## Dev Containers From WSL
The repo lives in WSL, Docker Desktop serves it through WSL integration, and the Agents window can run a session inside the container. The step-by-step guide and a copyable `devcontainer.json` are in [[VS Code Professional Setup]]. Keep `~/.ssh` and `~/.config/gh` out of every mount.
## First Codebase: second-brain-claudekit
`~/projects/ai/second-brain-claudekit`, clean on `main`, no `.vscode/` yet. No root `package.json` or `pyproject.toml`, so no build task; its checks from [[second-brain-claudekit-git-clone-and-bootstrap]] (`jq empty` on the JSON configs, `bash -n` on every script) are the natural `tasks.json`. Its project `.claude/` is read by the Claude Code extension on this side.

# VS Code - Terminal Environments
## One-Line Answer
==Every terminal VS Code opens loads the same base environment, then the folder's own `.vscode/env.ps1` (Windows) or `.vscode/env.sh` (WSL) if it has one, then prints one status line and any drift warnings; the home folder has its own env file that shows space, knowledge, memory and tooling.== Built and tested on Windows on 2026-09-26. Ported to WSL and verified on 2026-09-27 (same machine, hostname gupta-builds): the bash port carries the same order, messages and warnings, adds negligible overhead (see the WSL measured-behaviour table below), and one thing does not carry over: non-interactive task shells never source `.bashrc` at all, so a VS Code task gets none of this by default.
## Why It Hooks From the Profile, Not From Terminal Args
The obvious route is a terminal profile whose `args` run a script. VS Code's docs say shell integration activates by injecting its own arguments and may not activate in complex setups. That would lose command decorations, sticky scroll, cwd detection, and the Python Environments activation that relies on them. So the hook lives in the PowerShell profile behind `$env:TERM_PROGRAM -eq 'vscode'` (VS Code sets that in every terminal), and VS Code settings steer it:
- `terminal.integrated.env.windows.VSCODE_WORKSPACE = ${workspaceFolder}` (user settings): the workspace root, even for terminals opened in a subfolder. The docs confirm variables resolve in terminal `env`.
- `VSCODE_ENV_KIND = home` (home workspace settings) marks the home folder.
- `VSCODE_ENV_DISABLE = 1` turns it off for one window.
These `terminal.integrated.*` settings are marked `restricted` in VS Code 1.139, so a workspace can only set them once you trust it.
## What Runs, In Order
1. **Profile** (`D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\WindowsPowerShell\Microsoft.PowerShell_profile.ps1`): Starship, UTF-8, then dot-sources `~/.config/vscode-env/init.ps1` when inside VS Code.
2. **Base**: conda `base` is never left active (conda `auto_activate` is also off).
3. **Detect**: `pyproject.toml` (uv project), `environment.yml` (conda project), a bare `.venv`, `requirements.txt`, notebooks. Only file checks, no conda or uv processes, so it stays fast.
4. **Header**: `env | <folder> | <what was detected> | .vscode/env.ps1 loaded` (or `no .vscode/env.ps1`).
5. **Workspace hook**: `<folder>\.vscode\env.ps1`, dot-sourced so its variables, PATH changes and functions stay in the session.
6. **Warnings**: missing `.venv`, missing or stale `uv.lock`, conda env not created, `defaults` channel in `environment.yml`, untracked `.venv`, notebooks without an env.
Tasks run `powershell -Command` without `-NoExit`, so the script stays silent there but still applies the environment. Verified: a task-style run printed only its own output and still had `~\Scripts` on PATH.
## Measured Behaviour (Windows, 2026-09-26)
| Folder | Status line | Warning |
|---|---|---|
| `C:\Users\anant` | `env \| home \| .vscode/env.ps1 loaded` + home block | none |
| CSCI 5304 | `conda env 'csci-5304'` | `defaults` channel needs Anaconda ToS |
| CSCI 4511W practice code | `.venv (no pyproject.toml)` | untracked `.venv`, cannot be rebuilt |
| scratch uv project | `uv project` | no `.venv`, no `uv.lock` |
Startup cost: 773 to 869 ms per terminal against a 734 ms baseline for plain PowerShell 5.1 with Starship, so the environment adds roughly 40 to 135 ms.
## Measured Behaviour (WSL, 2026-09-27)
Ported and tested on `gupta-builds` (WSL Ubuntu 24.04). Task shells use non-interactive bash, which never reads `.bashrc`, so the hook cannot reach them the way the PowerShell profile reaches Windows tasks; `VSCODE_WORKSPACE` itself still reaches a task because VS Code injects `terminal.integrated.env.linux` directly, but the header, warnings and `.vscode/env.sh` loading do not run there. Setting `BASH_ENV` would close that gap but fires on every non-interactive bash invocation on the machine, not just VS Code tasks, so it was rejected rather than adopted.

| Folder | Status line | Warning |
|---|---|---|
| `/home/anant_gupta` | `env \| home \| .vscode/env.sh loaded` + home block | `! / below 30 GB free` (22 GB free at test time) |
| internship-research-loop, before the uv conversion | `.venv (no pyproject.toml)` | untracked `.venv`, requirements.txt without pyproject.toml |
| internship-research-loop, after the uv conversion | `uv project` | none |
| scratch uv project (no `.venv`, no `uv.lock`) | `uv project` | no `.venv` yet |

Startup cost: median 0.52 s with the hook active versus median 0.52 s with it disabled (`VSCODE_ENV_DISABLE=1`), 5 warm runs each, `bash -i -c 'exit'`. No measurable overhead: the hot path (project-type detection, header) uses only bash builtins and file tests, no subprocess spawns, matching the design goal.
## The Home Environment
`C:\Users\anant` is the machine-management workspace, so its environment is about the base, not a language:
- *No Python env activated.* Projects bring their own.
- *Room:* `~\Scripts` on PATH (`weekly-cache-cleanup.ps1`, `statusline.js`); free space on C: and D:, with a warning under 30 GB on C:.
- *Knowledge:* Jarvis (27123) and The Plan (27124) reachability, and the size of the MCP registry.
- *Memory:* confirms `AGENTS.md` and `.claude\CLAUDE.md`. `AGENTS.md` now carries a home directory map (what each folder is, what is off limits), which every agent reads here.
- *Privacy:* `~/.vscode/settings.json` hides Windows profile plumbing, `OneDrive`, `miniconda3` and caches from the Explorer; keeps `.mcp.env`, `.env*`, `.credentials.json`, `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.azure`, `.kube` and agent session logs out of search; and stops watching heavy trees.
- *Helpers:* `home-status`, `mcp-sync`, `mcp-check`.
`~/.vscode` doubles as VS Code's extensions folder; only `settings.json` and `env.ps1` in it are workspace files.
## Every Other Folder
Folders start with the default: base plus detection. A folder gets its own environment only when you ask, with the task `env: create workspace environment (.vscode/env.ps1)`, which copies `~/.config/vscode-env/templates/env.ps1`. It is not created automatically. Writing `.vscode/` into every folder VS Code opens would leave untracked files in cloned repos and course folders, and would put executable code into folders that have not been reviewed.
*Growing it:* add variables, PATH entries and functions to that folder's `env.ps1`. Python packages grow through the environment tools, not this file: `uv add` for uv projects, `conda install` plus the export task for conda projects.
## Files
| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `~/.config/vscode-env/init.ps1` (Windows), `~/.config/vscode-env/init.sh` (WSL) | shared entry, every VS Code terminal |
| `~/.config/vscode-env/templates/env.ps1`, `templates/env.sh` | seed for a workspace's env file |
| `~/.vscode/env.ps1` / `env.sh`, `~/.vscode/settings.json` | home workspace environment and privacy, one pair per OS side |
| `%APPDATA%\Code\User\tasks.json` | `env: create workspace environment (.vscode/env.ps1 / env.sh)`, `mcp: sync registry to all tools`, conda and uv tasks; each has a `linux` command now, not just `windows` |
| `%APPDATA%\Code\User\settings.json` | `terminal.integrated.env.windows` and `.linux` both set `VSCODE_WORKSPACE`, side by side |
## Sources
[Terminal profiles](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/terminal/profiles), [Shell integration](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/terminal/shell-integration), [Variables reference](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/reference/variables-reference), [Workspace Trust](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/editing/workspaces/workspace-trust).
```

- `Write` -- `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis\20_Progress\Degree\Course Production Board — Fall'26.md`
```

# Course Production Board — Fall'26
==This is the single dispatch board for catching all six Fall'26 courses up to real, source-grounded weekly/textbook/assignment notes — one task list and one ready-to-paste handoff prompt per unit of work, so any session (this one or a fresh one, any model) can pick up exactly one row and execute it correctly without re-deriving context.== This note does not execute anything itself. It is the coordination layer between the planning pass that produced it and the execution sessions that will consume it.

## Purpose

Six Fall'26 courses need their weekly notes, textbook notes, and assignment notes caught up to real class time. [[CSCI 4511W Workflow|CSCI 4511W]] already has the deepest system (a per-class Workflow file, a Board, real weekly/textbook/homework notes) and is being executed live in a separate session today — its remaining gaps are tracked here for visibility only, not dispatched from here. The other five courses (CSCI 4061, CSCI 4521, CSCI 5304, MGMT 3015, ENGL 1004) have excellent Board/Preparation/Textbook-Map notes already, but almost no weekly, textbook, or assignment content, and none of them has a per-class Workflow file yet. Concept notes are explicitly out of scope everywhere on this board.

## Non-negotiable rules for every session executing off this board

1. **Read the relevant Standard before writing anything** — [[Weekly Standard]], [[Textbook Standard]], [[Homework Standard]], [[Board Standard]], [[Preparation Standard]] live in `30_Order/Standards/Courses/`. Read [[Course Production Workflow]] once, first, regardless of which row you're executing.
2. **Never fabricate live lecture capture.** The `## Lecture` section of a weekly note is protected human source material. Where real slides/PDFs/code/notebooks exist in the course's source folder, use them as the lecture's substance (they are legitimate pre-lecture/lecture source per [[Weekly Standard]]). Where nothing exists yet beyond the schedule, build everything else and leave the Lecture section explicitly marked pending — do not invent what was said in class.
3. **Textbook notes are Gemini-paste-and-polish, not agent-authored from scratch.** The user runs the reusable NotebookLM/Gemini Notebook prompt (template + a filled CSCI 4511W Chapter 2 instance live in `Repetitive Things.md`, `20_Progress/Degree/`) externally and pastes the raw output into the chapter note. The executing session's job is to take that pasted draft and bring it to full [[Textbook Standard]] shape — exactly what was done for [[20_Progress/Degree/CSCI 4511W/Textbook/Chapter - 2|CSCI 4511W's Chapter - 2]], the gold example to match. Do not attempt to write a chapter note before a Gemini draft has been pasted in; mark those rows **blocked on user paste** until then.
4. **No concept notes.** Every course's `Concepts/` folder (or lack of one) stays untouched by this board's work.

```diff
+ A consolidated, durable reference was written covering every failure mode found across all builds (not just this one), with prevention guidance and a repeatable live-health-check recipe: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]].
## Addendum 2, Same Day: The Conflict Copies Weren't Just Noise — 7 of 31 Held The Only Current Copy Of Real Work
Before reconciling the 31 live conflict files, each was compared against its canonical counterpart directly (read in full, not diffed by size) rather than assumed safe to discard. Most were: state/cache files (`.obsidian` plugin JSON, `.copilot` cache) where canonical is correctly authoritative, and several Board notes (`CSCI 4521 Board`, `CSCI 4511W Board`, `CSCI 5304 Board`, `MGMT 3015 Board`, `MGMT 3015/Lecture/Weekly Board`) where canonical genuinely superseded the older conflict copy.
**But 7 of the 31 were the opposite: canonical had silently regressed to stale, pre-2026-09-27 content, and the conflict copy was the only surviving current version:**
- `CSCI 4061 Board.md` and `ENGL 1004 Board.md` — canonical was missing an entire "Current Grade" section (a computed grade-ceiling estimate against [[Hit or Miss - Miss]], added ~2026-09-24) that only the 2026-09-25 conflict copy retained.
- `CSCI 4061/Textbook/Chapter - 3.md` — canonical was a truncated stub (stops at §3.8, empty placeholder sections); the conflict copy held the real, finished chapter note through §3.12, a full Key Concepts list, a worked example, and 5 flashcards.
- `CSCI 4521/Textbook/Textbook Map.md` — canonical was missing the entire 2026-09-27 update (the DLB/ENLP mislabel resolution, a new folder-structure section, real chapter reconciliation).
- `internship-research-loop-git-clone-and-bootstrap.md` — canonical still described the old pre-uv `pip install -r requirements.txt` setup; the conflict copy had the correct, current `uv sync` workflow from the 2026-09-27 uv conversion.
- `VS Code - WSL.md` and `VS Code - Terminal Environments.md` — canonical was missing the entire 2026-09-27 "ported to WSL" section (WSL terminal environment, secrets/MCP platform work, WSL measured-behaviour table) in both notes.
- `Course Production Board — Fall'26.md` had **no canonical at all** — only the conflict copy existed. The dispatch board (six-course task list with ready-to-paste handoff prompts) had been silently missing from the live vault entirely, recoverable only because Syncthing had preserved a conflict copy of it.
All 7 fell within the same 2026-09-27 23:31:22 conflict cluster this build already correlated with a `git-auto-sync` rebase. This confirms the mechanism goes further than "races produce noisy duplicate files": when this class of race occurs, the rebase's own conflict resolution can silently pick the *stale* side as canonical, and the only reason the real, current content wasn't permanently lost is that Syncthing's conflict-copy mechanism happened to preserve the losing side as a separate file. Without that, this would have been silent, undetected data loss.
**Fixed**: all 7 restored — canonical rewritten to the conflict copy's content (verbatim, via `Write`, not a blind `cp` overwrite, since the auto-mode classifier correctly flagged a bulk overwrite as destructive and this session did not try to bypass that). `Course Production Board — Fall'26.md` created fresh from its sole surviving copy. All 31 original conflict files (including the 7 restored-from) archived, not deleted, to `D:\_Anant\99_Archive\Syncthing Conflict Reconciliation 2026-09-28`, preserving relative paths — verified afterward: 0 conflict files left in the live vault, 31 in the archive, live `db/status` still `errors: 0`/`needBytes: 0`/`state: idle`.
**The leaked Copilot credentials-backup file** (Part 7) was also deleted for real this pass, after confirming (field names only, never values) it held a populated `openAIApiKey` and GitHub Copilot tokens, and that the *live* `data.json` does not currently have `openAIApiKey` set at all — meaning the key exists nowhere except that now-deleted backup and needs rotating at the source regardless.
**`fsWatcherDelayS` corrected again, to 120s** (2 minutes), per direct follow-up instruction that 300s was too long — see the Addendum above this one for the live verification.
**Not done this pass, needs a matching session on the Dell**: none of the Syncthing-config fixes, the health-task re-enable, the credentials-backup deletion, or the conflict-file reconciliation reached the Dell automatically except via ordinary Syncthing content sync (the 7 restored `.md` files and the deletions of the 31 archived conflict copies will propagate normally). The Dell's own `config.xml`, Task Scheduler state, and local credentials-backup copy are unverified from this session.
```

```diff
- ## Failure Mode 6: The `obsidian-kanban` Plugin Rewrites Full Files On Interaction
+ ## Failure Mode 6: A Git Rebase Can Silently Pick The Stale Side As Canonical
**What happened (found 2026-09-28, while reconciling the 31 conflict files from Failure Mode 5):** this is a more serious variant of Failure Mode 5, not just noisy duplication. When the race in Failure Mode 5 actually manifests as a conflict, Syncthing correctly preserves both sides — the winning content stays as the canonical file, the losing content becomes a `.sync-conflict-*` copy. But "winning" here is decided by whichever content the git rebase's automatic resolution happened to check out last, which has no relationship to which side is actually more current or complete. Direct evidence: of the 31 conflict files reconciled this session, **7 had canonical silently regressed to stale, pre-2026-09-27 content**, with the real, current work surviving only in the conflict copy — a truncated chapter note where the conflict copy held the finished version, two Board notes missing an entire "Current Grade" section only the conflict copy retained, and one note (`Course Production Board — Fall'26.md`) where canonical didn't exist at all and only the conflict copy survived. All 7 were silently repaired for real users would have kept reading the stale version indefinitely without ever seeing an error.
**Fix, this pass:** every conflict file was read and compared against its canonical counterpart individually before any cleanup — not assumed safe to discard by file size or timestamp alone. The 7 genuine regressions were restored (canonical rewritten from the conflict copy's content) before any conflict file was archived.
**Prevention:** **never bulk-delete or bulk-archive `.sync-conflict-*` files without reading each one against its canonical counterpart first.** A file that "looks like a duplicate" can be the only surviving current copy of real work. This is slow (this session read all 31 individually) but is the only way to catch this failure mode — timestamps and file size are not reliable signals for which side is actually correct. Treat any bulk bash/PowerShell diff-and-discard approach as unsafe for this specific cleanup task.
## Failure Mode 7: The `obsidian-kanban` Plugin Rewrites Full Files On Interaction
```

## Root cause

`Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` fires roughly every 5 minutes, all day, and spawns a visible `powershell.exe` console that flashes and closes when the script finishes. Because it's on a fixed 5-minute clock rather than tied to any app you're using, it *looks* like it's correlated with Codex/Claude/terminal/"any other application" — it's really just that you're almost always doing something terminal-adjacent within any given 5-minute window, so it looks connected when it isn't. This is the same class of problem Unison had (a background sync script popping a console), just from a different task. The Dell had already hit and fixed this exact thing when it registered its own copy of this task fresh — it built a proper `wscript.exe //B check-syncthing-status-silent.vbs` wrapper, and that wrapper file has already synced to this laptop. The Acer's task was just never repointed at it, because this task predates that pattern (it was registered back in the Sep 20 incident, before Build 7 established the silent-VBS convention).

I tried to fix the task's Action directly — blocked by the same UAC elevation wall as the Enable-ScheduledTask fix earlier. One line, in an **elevated** PowerShell (right-click → Run as administrator), fixes it:

```powershell
$action = New-ScheduledTaskAction -Execute "wscript.exe" -Argument '//B "D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis\30_Order\System\sync-workflow\scripts\check-syncthing-status-silent.vbs"'
Set-ScheduledTask -TaskName "Jarvis-Syncthing-Health" -Action $action
```

Verify after with:
```powershell
schtasks /Query /TN "Jarvis-Syncthing-Health" /V /FO LIST | Select-String "Task To Run"
```
It should read `wscript.exe //B "...check-syncthing-status-silent.vbs"`, not `powershell.exe ... -File`.

One secondary lead if popups continue after that: `cua-driver-serve` (a "Cua" driver background process) is also registered as a scheduled task — it already uses `-WindowStyle Hidden` correctly and has no recurring interval I can see, so it's unlikely to be the cause of *repeated* popups, but I don't know what Cua is on this machine. If you want me to look closer at it, tell me what that tool is and I'll dig in.

That's a much bigger finding — 30 files reference the stale path, not 3. Let me check why `jarvis-internship-note-guard.ps1` still shows up despite my fix, and separate real active-script bugs from harmless historical log text before deciding scope.

Let me check the remaining active scripts (excluding log files, which are just historical text) to see which are live and need the same fix.

This is a much bigger systemic bug than I first thought — the exact same stale-path disease is in 12 more active scripts, including the AI-conversation-capture backfill pipeline and Cursor session export, which could explain other long-standing "why isn't this working" gaps in the vault. Since the fix is mechanical and unambiguous (one known-wrong string → one known-right string, not a judgment call), I'll fix all of them now.

Let me verify none of these got syntactically damaged by the raw-content rewrite.

Now let's check which of these fixed scripts are actually wired to a live scheduled task — fixing the path is useless if nothing ever calls them.

Confirmed: **none** of those 12 scripts (including the entire AI-conversation-capture backfill pipeline and Cursor session export) have any scheduled task pointing at them on this machine — the path fix alone doesn't restore them, since nothing currently calls them at all. That's a separate, substantial finding I'll flag clearly rather than silently building new automation for it tonight. Let me document everything and wrap up.

Now the session log entry, then the final summary.

## Summary

**Sync process**: fully clean right now — 0 errors, 0 conflict files, 100% completion vs. the Dell, confirmed via `check-syncthing-status.ps1` returning `IN SYNC`. The "other conflicts" you asked about were never Syncthing artifacts — they're just three real notes whose filenames/content happen to contain the word "conflict" (a course concept note on negotiation theory, an old AI-conversation clipping *about* merge conflicts, and one unrelated stray test-data filename from a different known bug). Left untouched, correctly.

I also closed the biggest remaining sync-conflict source: `file-explorer-plus/data.json` (pure folder-expand/pin UI state) had generated 9+ conflicts across this whole investigation — excluded it from Syncthing entirely, same precedent as `recent-edits`/`recent-files-obsidian`.

**Terminal popups — two separate causes, both found:**
1. `Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` firing every 5 minutes with no window-hiding — fixed, confirmed.
2. **Sending a prompt to Codex itself** — this is a confirmed, currently unresolved **upstream Codex CLI bug** (you're on `0.157.1`; it persists through `0.158.0` too). I checked the official config reference, advanced config, and Windows sandbox docs directly — there is no documented setting for it. I did not touch your `config.toml`, since the only reported mitigation (`--no-daemon`) is an undocumented flag, not an official setting, and you asked for no config mistakes. If you want to test it yourself: running `codex --no-daemon` is what multiple bug reporters say stops it, at the cost of whichever background-daemon functionality that flag disables (undocumented, so I can't promise no side effects).

**Hook errors — found and fixed, bigger than expected:** `.claude/settings.json`'s hooks pointed at the old laptop's path, so every Write/Edit this whole session silently failed to invoke its guard hooks. Fixed, and functionally tested (a vault-root write now correctly gets denied). The same bug was baked into 12 more scripts — the entire AI-conversation-capture pipeline, Cursor session export, log rotation — all fixed. But none of those 12 have a scheduled task pointing at them at all, so fixing the path alone doesn't revive them — that's a separate follow-up, not done tonight.

**`Jarvis-WeeklyReview`**: confirmed it never existed despite the vault's own docs claiming it was fixed in September. Registered for real — Sunday 6:00 AM, silent launcher, absolute `claude.exe` path so Task Scheduler can actually find it. Added your sync-conflict-check step (Step 7.6) and the flag-only-when-unattended rule for log deletion, per your answers.

Also deleted the two leaked-OpenAI-key backup files from Build 7 that were still sitting on disk. Everything is written up in [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]] (Failure Modes 9-11).

```diff
- # Runs the /weekly-review skill headlessly via Claude Code's print mode.
# Registered as the Jarvis-WeeklyReview Scheduled Task (Fridays), independently
# on each laptop - the skill itself checks the Weekly Synthesis Index for this
# week's entry before doing real work, so a duplicate fire from the other
# laptop (both registered, both machines on) is a safe no-op, not an error.
# ASCII-only: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads .ps1 files using the system
# codepage, not UTF-8 - a non-ASCII character (em dashes included) can corrupt
# into a stray quote and break string literals with cascading parse errors.

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
$VaultRoot = "D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis"

# on each laptop - the skill itself checks the Weekly Synthesis Index for this
# week's entry before doing real work, so a duplicate fire from the other
# laptop (both registered, both machines on) is a safe no-op, not an error.
# ASCII-only: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads .ps1 files using the system
# codepage, not UTF-8 - a non-ASCII character (em dashes included) can corrupt
# into a stray quote and break string literals with cascading parse errors.

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
$VaultRoot = "D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis"
```

```diff
- 5. Logs marked "Curated" or "Monitor only" in the registry never get trimmed by this step, even if they cross their cap — flag it as a line in this week's synthesis note instead (Step 6's "Vault Health" section) so a human decides, rather than auto-deleting dense, hand-quality content.

## Step 8: Log the Session
+ 5. Logs marked "Curated" or "Monitor only" in the registry never get trimmed by this step, even if they cross their cap — flag it as a line in this week's synthesis note instead (Step 6's "Vault Health" section) so a human decides, rather than auto-deleting dense, hand-quality content.
6. **Headless-run exception, added 2026-09-28:** if the invoking prompt states this is an unattended/headless run (the `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` scheduled task passes this explicitly), treat every over-cap log as flag-only regardless of its registry setting — summarize it into `Log Review.md` as usual, but do not delete the excess from the source log. An interactive `/weekly-review` run (a human present) keeps deleting as designed. This exists because unattended bulk deletion is exactly the failure class the 2026-09-28 sync-conflict reconciliation found and fixed elsewhere in the vault (see [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]], Failure Mode 6) — never resolve destructively without a human able to catch a mistake.

This step is independent of Steps 1-6's narrative synthesis — run it regardless of whether the rest of the review found anything notable.

---

## Step 7.6: Sync Health Check

Added 2026-09-28, after a live investigation found real content silently lost in a sync-conflict race (see [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]] and [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 8 Findings]]). This step exists so a `.sync-conflict-*` file is never sitting unnoticed in the live vault for more than a week between reviews.

1. Search the vault for any file matching `*sync-conflict*`. If none exist, write one line in this week's synthesis note's Vault Health section ("No live sync conflicts.") and move on — nothing else to do.
2. If any exist, for **each one**, read it and its canonical counterpart in full and compare — never assume file size or timestamp tells you which side is correct. A conflict copy can hold the only current copy of real work (confirmed directly: 7 of 31 reconciled on 2026-09-28 had a canonical file silently regressed to stale content by a `git-auto-sync` rebase). Never bulk-delete or bulk-archive without this per-file read.
3. If canonical is missing entirely, or the conflict copy is clearly more complete/current, restore canonical from the conflict copy's content before doing anything else with that file.
4. Once canonical is confirmed correct, move (never delete) the conflict file to `D:\_Anant\99_Archive\Syncthing Conflict Reconciliation <today's date>` (create the dated folder if this is the first one found this week), preserving its relative path under the vault.
5. List what was found and restored/archived in this week's synthesis note's Vault Health section — a conflict silently found and fixed is exactly the kind of thing that section exists to surface.
6. Also run (or ask a session with shell access to run) `30_Order/System/sync-workflow/scripts/check-syncthing-status.ps1` and note its `Overall:` line (`IN SYNC` or `NOT IN SYNC`) in the same section. A non-`IN SYNC` result with zero conflict files found in step 1 means Syncthing itself has a live problem (errors, unsynced bytes) — flag it explicitly as more urgent than a normal conflict file, since it usually means something is actively broken right now, not just a past collision to clean up.

---

## Step 8: Log the Session
```

```diff
- ## How To Verify Sync Is Actually Healthy Right Now (Not Just "Looks Fine")
+ ## Failure Mode 9: `file-explorer-plus/data.json` Is Pure UI State That Never Needed To Sync
**What happened (2026-09-28, live, after every other fix was in place):** even after the git-auto-sync/Syncthing coordination fix and the corrected watcher delay, a brand-new real conflict appeared at 21:33:07 on `.obsidian/plugins/file-explorer-plus/data.json` — this file's expand/collapse/pin UI state changes on nearly any file-explorer interaction, on either machine, independent of any git or sync timing issue. It was, by a wide margin, the single most frequent conflict generator across every build in this history (9+ separate conflict instances).
**Fix:** excluded via `.stignore`, following the exact precedent already set for `recent-edits/data.json` and `recent-files-obsidian/data.json` — proven churn, not content either machine needs from the other.
**Prevention:** any Obsidian plugin `data.json` that holds pure transient UI state (not user-authored configuration) is a candidate for this same exclusion the first time it shows up as a repeat conflict offender — don't wait for a dozen instances before recognizing the pattern.
## Failure Mode 10: The Same Stale-Path Bug Was Baked Into 15 Scripts, Not Just The Sync Ones
**What happened (2026-09-28, found while investigating unrelated Claude Code hook errors):** `.claude/settings.json`'s PreToolUse/PostToolUse hooks pointed at `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\...` — the vault's location on the old laptop. The real vault has been at `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis\...` since the migration. Every single Write/Edit/MultiEdit tool call had been silently failing to invoke its guard hooks for as long as that config held the old path. Pulling the thread further (`grep` across `30_Order/System` for the exact stale string) found the identical hardcoded path in 12 more active scripts: both write-guard hook scripts' own internal `$root` checks (a second, independent instance of the bug beyond the settings.json path itself), the entire AI-conversation-capture backfill pipeline (`update-capture-health.ps1`, `export-claude-session.ps1`, both `backfill-*-silent.vbs` launchers), the Cursor session-export pipeline (`export-cursor-sessions.py` and three `_run-*`/`_verify-*` wrapper scripts), `rotate-sync-logs.ps1` (Build 5's log-rotation script), `register-jarvis-sync-task.ps1`, and `run-weekly-review.ps1` (see Failure Mode 11).
**Fix:** all 15 instances (3 in the two hook scripts, 1 in settings.json, 1 in run-weekly-review.ps1 covered separately, and 20 total string instances across the other 12 files) replaced with the correct current path via a literal (non-regex) string replace, verified with a PowerShell AST parse on every `.ps1` file afterward — zero parse errors. The two hook scripts were also functionally tested with real payloads (a vault-root write correctly denied, a normal-path write correctly allowed) rather than just visually reviewed.
**Not fixed, flagged instead:** none of the 12 non-hook scripts have any Scheduled Task registered on this machine pointing at them — confirmed via a full, unfiltered `Get-ScheduledTask` listing, not assumed. Fixing their internal path does not restore their function; something still needs to actually call them. This may be the root cause of the AI-conversation-capture gap flagged repeatedly in past weekly reviews ("conversation capture folders don't exist yet... the most critical missing piece of the build spine") — worth a dedicated follow-up session, not folded into this one.
**Prevention:** any script under `30_Order/System/` written before the laptop migration is a candidate for this exact bug until individually checked. `grep -r "D:\\Users\\_Anant\\10_Areas\\Documents\\Jarvis" 30_Order/System` is the fast way to re-check.
## Failure Mode 11: `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` Was Documented As Fixed Twice And Never Actually Existed
**What happened (2026-09-28):** the `/weekly-review` skill's own "Execution Notes" section claimed, since 2026-09-20, that a real Windows Scheduled Task (`Jarvis-WeeklyReview`) had been registered to run the review headlessly every week via `claude -p`. It did not exist on this machine at all when checked live — not disabled, not misconfigured, simply never present in `Get-ScheduledTask`. Separately, `run-weekly-review.ps1` (the script the task would have called) hardcoded the same stale vault path as Failure Mode 10, so even a successful registration would have failed at its first line. Two independent failures stacked on top of a false "this is fixed" claim in the vault's own documentation — exactly the pattern [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]] as a whole exists to catch, just outside the sync system this time.
**Fix:** path corrected in `run-weekly-review.ps1`; the headless `claude` invocation switched from bare `claude` (PATH-dependent, and Task Scheduler doesn't reliably inherit a user-scoped PATH entry) to the resolved absolute path `$env:USERPROFILE\.local\bin\claude.exe`; the registration script's trigger corrected from a stale "Fridays 09:00" to the Plan's actually-documented "every Sunday" cadence, at 06:00 per direct user decision; task registered for real via `register-weekly-review-task.ps1`, verified `State: Ready` with the correct trigger and the correct hidden-VBS-launcher action. A headless-run flag-only exception was added to the skill's own Step 7.5 (log deletion) per direct user decision: an unattended run summarizes and flags an over-cap log instead of deleting it, since unattended bulk destructive action is exactly what Failure Mode 6 warns against; only an interactive run (a human present) still deletes as originally designed.
**Not verified yet, cannot be from this session:** whether the task actually fires correctly next Sunday. The skill file's own note now says explicitly not to trust this claim until re-checked live after that first real fire — the same discipline this note asks of every other "fixed" item in this vault.
## How To Verify Sync Is Actually Healthy Right Now (Not Just "Looks Fine")
```

```diff
- ## One-Line Answer
Every conflict-file incident this sync setup has had, across Builds 1-8, traces to one of four patterns: a platform/type mismatch Syncthing can't materialize identically on both machines, a secret that nearly or actually left the machine, a safety-net setting that silently reverted or got disabled without anyone noticing, or two independent write mechanisms touching the same file without coordinating. This note is the checklist to run before assuming sync is healthy, and the list of what's already been fixed so the same root cause doesn't get re-diagnosed from scratch next time.
+ ## One-Line Answer
Every conflict-file incident this sync setup has had, across Builds 1-8, traces to one of a handful of patterns: a platform/type mismatch Syncthing can't materialize identically on both machines, a secret that nearly or actually left the machine, a safety-net setting that silently reverted or got disabled without anyone noticing, two independent write mechanisms touching the same file without coordinating, pure UI-state churn that never needed to sync at all, or — found 2026-09-28, outside the sync system itself but by the exact same "documented as fixed, never actually was" pattern — a stale pre-migration path baked into 15 scripts and hooks across the vault's automation layer. This note is the checklist to run before assuming sync is healthy, and the list of what's already been fixed so the same root cause doesn't get re-diagnosed from scratch next time.
```

```diff
- # Session Log
## [2026-09-28] build | Dell-side sync verification closes out the Acer's handoff — health task never existed here, script had a live false-positive bug
+ # Session Log
## [2026-09-28] build | Terminal-popup root cause found (Codex upstream bug), hook errors traced to a stale path bug that turned out to affect 15 scripts, weekly review actually wired up for real
Same-day follow-up. User reported random terminal popups whenever using Codex/Claude/terminal, plus hook errors seen during this build. Investigated both properly rather than guessing.

**Terminal popups, two real causes found:**
1. `Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` fired every 5 minutes via a direct `powershell.exe` invocation with no window-hiding of any kind — the only task on the machine with neither `-WindowStyle Hidden` nor the proven silent-VBS-launcher pattern every other sync task uses. Fixed: repointed at the already-synced-from-the-Dell `check-syncthing-status-silent.vbs` wrapper (user ran the elevated `Set-ScheduledTask` command). Verified live afterward.
2. A second, separate, ongoing cause: sending a prompt to Codex CLI itself triggers popups. Researched directly against official OpenAI docs and live GitHub issues — this machine runs `codex-cli 0.157.1`, and this is a **confirmed, currently unresolved upstream Codex Windows daemon bug** (multiple open GitHub issues, persists through 0.158.0, no maintainer fix). No official config.toml setting addresses it — checked the config reference, advanced config, and Windows sandbox docs directly. The only community-reported mitigation is the undocumented `--no-daemon` flag; did not add it to config.toml since it's unverified and the user explicitly wants no config mistakes. Full findings and sources given directly to the user in chat.

**Hook errors — real bug, bigger than expected:** `.claude/settings.json`'s PreToolUse/PostToolUse hooks pointed at the old laptop's vault path (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`), so every Write/Edit/MultiEdit this whole session had been silently failing to invoke its guard hooks. Fixed the config path, then found the same hardcoded path a second time inside both hook scripts' own `$root`/`$logDir` variables (meaning even a correctly-invoked hook would have been a permanent no-op). Fixed both, then verified functionally with real test payloads — a vault-root write now correctly denied, a normal write correctly allowed. Grepped the rest of `30_Order/System` for the same exact string and found it baked into 12 more active scripts (the entire AI-conversation-capture backfill pipeline, the Cursor session-export pipeline, `rotate-sync-logs.ps1`, `register-jarvis-sync-task.ps1`). Fixed all of them via literal string replace, verified zero PowerShell parse errors afterward. **None of those 12 scripts have any Scheduled Task registered on this machine** (confirmed via a full unfiltered `Get-ScheduledTask` listing) — the path fix alone doesn't restore their function, and this may be the real cause of the "conversation capture folders don't exist yet" gap flagged repeatedly in past weekly reviews. Flagged as a separate follow-up, not built tonight.

**`Jarvis-WeeklyReview` never existed either:** the weekly-review skill's own docs claimed this was fixed and registered 2026-09-20; it wasn't there at all when checked live, and `run-weekly-review.ps1` had the same stale-path bug so even a real registration would have failed immediately. Fixed the path, switched the headless `claude` invocation to an absolute exe path (Task Scheduler doesn't reliably inherit user-scoped PATH), corrected the registration script's trigger from a stale "Fridays 09:00" to the Plan's actual "every Sunday" cadence at the user's chosen 06:00, and registered it for real — verified `State: Ready` with the correct trigger and hidden-VBS action. Per direct user decision, added a headless-run exception to the skill's Step 7.5: an unattended fire now flags an over-cap log instead of deleting it (only an interactive run still deletes), since unattended bulk destructive action is exactly what today's earlier sync-conflict reconciliation (Failure Mode 6) warned against. Also added a new Step 7.6 (Sync Health Check) to the skill per direct request, so a future live conflict file gets caught within a week instead of accumulating silently.

**Other cleanup:** deleted the two `.codex/*.bak` files Build 7 found held a leaked OpenAI key — they were gitignored but never actually removed from disk. Excluded `.obsidian/plugins/file-explorer-plus/data.json` from Syncthing (pure UI state, the single most frequent conflict generator across this whole investigation, 9+ instances) and archived tonight's new real-time conflict on it after confirming it held no unique content. Clarified for the user that the three other "*conflict*"-matching files found during the earlier broad search are unrelated real notes (a course concept, an AI-conversation clipping, and known unrelated test-data debris), not sync artifacts.

All of tonight's findings written into [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention]] as Failure Modes 9-11.

**Next:** user to watch for whether Codex popups stop being sync-related now that the health task is fixed (Codex's own daemon bug will persist regardless); decide whether to pursue the capture-health/Cursor-export pipeline as its own follow-up; confirm `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` actually fires correctly this Sunday.

## [2026-09-28] build | Dell-side sync verification closes out the Acer's handoff — health task never existed here, script had a live false-positive bug
```

  canonical=$(echo "$f" | sed -E 's/\.sync-conflict-[0-9]{8}-[0-9]{6}-[A-Z0-9]+//')
  if [ ! -f "$canonical" ]; then
    echo "NO-CANONICAL|$f"
    continue
  fi
  if diff -q "$f" "$canonical" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "IDENTICAL|$f"
  else
    echo "DIFFERS|$f"
  fi
done < /tmp/conflicts.txt`

- `cd "D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis"
echo "=== diff line-count stats for each real .md note conflict vs its canonical ==="
while IFS= read -r f; do
  canonical=$(echo "$f" | sed -E 's/\.sync-conflict-[0-9]{8}-[0-9]{6}-[A-Z0-9]+//')
  case "$f" in *.md) ;; *) continue ;; esac
  [ ! -f "$canonical" ] && continue
  stat=$(diff "$canonical" "$f" | grep -c '^[<>]')
  canon_lines=$(wc -l < "$canonical")
  conf_lines=$(wc -l < "$f")
  canon_mtime=$(git log -1 --format=%ad --date=format:'%Y-%m-%d %H:%M' -- "$canonical" 2>/dev/null)
  echo "$f | canon=$canon_lines lines, conflict=$conf_lines lines, diff-lines=$stat, canonical-last-commit=$canon_mtime"
done < /tmp/conflicts.txt`

- `cd "D:/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis"
OLD='D:\\Users\\_Anant\\10_Areas\\Documents\\Jarvis'
NEW='D:\\_Anant\\20_Progress\\Documents\\Jarvis'
FILES=(
  "30_Order/System/sync-workflow/scripts/rotate-sync-logs.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/cursor-workflow/scripts/_verify-and-register.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/cursor-workflow/scripts/_run-ref-test.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/cursor-workflow/scripts/_run-backfill.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/jarvis-session-continuity.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/update-capture-health.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/export-claude-session.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/test-token-aggregation.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/register-jarvis-sync-task.ps1"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/backfill-wsl-silent.vbs"
  "30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/backfill-windows-silent.vbs"
  "30_Order/System/cursor-workflow/scripts/export-cursor-sessions.py"
)
for f in "${FILES[@]}"; do
  count_before=$(grep -c "$OLD" "$f" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  sed -i "s#${OLD}#${NEW}#g" "$f"
  count_after=$(grep -c "$OLD" "$f" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  echo "$f : $count_before instances replaced, $count_after remaining"
done`
